Irritable? Oh, my... well, I really try so hard to be pleasant with everyone, you know? I don't want you to think I'm a difficult person. But... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest... maybe a little bit?

Worry about the same things? Oh, I... well, I suppose everyone worries a little bit, don't they? I mean, with managing the properties and making sure everything is running smoothly, there's always something to keep an eye on.

Preoccupied? Oh, um... well, no, I don't think so. I mean, my mind stays pretty busy, of course, with the properties and making sure the tenants are happy and all the maintenance is scheduled. You have to stay on top of those things, you know?

I really try to keep my thoughts productive. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I do that. I mean, if I'm ever concerned about something, there's always a real reason for it, like a plumbing issue at one of the units or a contractor running late. 

I wouldn't just sit around worrying about... well, nothing. That just seems like a silly waste of energy, doesn't it? I always try to be very practical. If there's no obvious reason to fret, I just go about my day and focus on what needs to get done. I definitely wouldn't want you to think I'm the sort of person who just imagines problems out of thin air. I try to be very sensible about it all. 

I hope that's the right way to handle things? I just want to be sure I'm doing okay.

I think everyone worries about their job a little. \n\nI'm definitely not, like, a nervous wreck or anything. I hope it's alright that I don't have a better answer for you. I just... I don't want to make a big deal out of nothing. Well, um... I suppose, maybe just a little bit lately? But it's probably just because I've been so busy with the properties, you know?

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I guess this really noticeable shift—you know, where I first had that burst of energy and felt like I could take on the world, and then suddenly dropped into this... this low, cloudy sort of fog—it's been about two weeks now. 

It started right around the time my husband retired. Suddenly he was just... home all day, every day. It’s been a bit of an adjustment, I suppose. I mean, I love having him around, of course! I don't want to sound like a terrible wife complaining about her husband. It's just... a big change to our routine, and maybe I haven't handled the transition as perfectly as I should have. 

Two weeks isn't that long though, right? I'm hoping I'll bounce back and get my focus back soon. I really am trying my best to push through it. Do you think that's a normal reaction to such a big life change?

Well, um... I guess I sort of mentioned this a minute ago, but... yes, I suppose it has been most of the day, nearly every day lately. 

Like I said, I just haven't wanted to go to my dance class at all, or even just take a walk to look at the little galleries downtown. And the Sunday dinners... Well, um... to be completely honest with you, I suppose these sort of... depressed stretches... they've actually been coming back on and off for about a year now. Um... no, not really. I mean, I worry a little bit about doing a good job at work, just making sure the properties are taken care of and the residents are happy. But that's just normal, right? I think everyone worries about their job a little. \n\nI'm definitely not, like, a nervous wreck or anything. I hope it's alright that I don't have a better answer for you. I just... I don't want to make a big deal out of nothing. Am I explaining this okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing or concentrating on things lately?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think that's exactly it. Um, like I was mentioning earlier... it's just that cloudy feeling. I'll be looking right at a vendor email, or one of those board packets, and my brain just sort of... stalls out, I guess? \n\nI just can't seem to concentrate enough to make a simple choice on things I used to handle real fast. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. Does that sound like what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble with your memory lately?\n\nPatient: Memory? Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I might misplace my keys every now and then, or forget why I walked into the kitchen, but at my age, I figure that's just part of getting older, right? \n\nI haven't forgotten any big things, like paying the bills or important dates. My memory seems pretty normal, I think. I hope that's helpful? I just want to make sure I'm answering these questions the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling more tired than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Tired? Well, um... no, I wouldn't say more than usual. I mean, sure, by the time the evening rolls around I'm ready to sit down, especially if I had to walk the grounds at one of the properties. But I think anyone my age gets a little worn out after a full day of work, right? \n\nI'm certainly not laying in bed all day or anything bad like that. I still get up and do what I need to do every morning with plenty of energy. I hope that's okay? I just... I want to make sure I'm giving you a good picture of how I'm doing. Is that the kind of answer you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Irritable? Oh, my... well, I really try so hard to be pleasant with everyone, you know? I don't want you to think I'm a difficult person. But... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest... maybe a little bit? \n\nI feel awful even admitting it. It's just... there were a few days recently where I felt almost, um, too bright and bold, I guess you could say. I'd be running the association meetings and I'd catch myself just talking right over people. Which really isn't like me at all! And then I'd get home and... well, I'd end up snapping at my husband over some tiny little thing. \n\nI always make sure to apologize right away, of course, because I hate being short with him. I hope it's alright that I'm telling you this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: Enjoying things? Oh, well... I mean, I try to. I feel terrible even saying it, because I know my family looks forward to it, but I just haven't had the desire to cook our big Sunday dinners recently. And... well, I usually love going to my dance class and walking through the little art galleries in town. Those are the things that usually make me feel like myself, you know? But lately, I just... haven't wanted to go. \n\nI know I should, and I'm going to try to get back to it soon, I promise. I don't want to sound like I'm just giving up or being lazy. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless? Oh, no, no. I wouldn't say hopeless. I mean, I know things can get a little... overwhelming sometimes, but I always try to look on the bright side, you know? \n\nI really believe things will get better. I mean, they have to, right? I have my husband, and the properties to manage, and... well, I just try to keep a positive attitude about everything. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. That's not me at all. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I just... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea about how I'm looking at things. I'm really trying my best to stay optimistic. Oh, I... well, I suppose everyone worries a little bit, don't they? Not to the point where it's a problem or anything like that. I really try to just handle whatever comes up and move on. I don't want to be one of those people who just frets all day long. Does that sound okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself preoccupied with certain thoughts that you can't seem to shake off?\n\nPatient: Preoccupied? Oh, um... well, no, I don't think so. I mean, my mind stays pretty busy, of course, with the properties and making sure the tenants are happy and all the maintenance is scheduled. You have to stay on top of those things, you know? \n\nBut I wouldn't say I'm preoccupied in a bad way, or stuck on things I can't shake off. I try very hard to just deal with a problem when it comes up and then put it out of my mind. I don't like to dwell on things. I think it's so much healthier to just keep moving forward, don't you? \n\nI really try to keep my thoughts productive. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really don't think I do that. I mean, if I'm ever concerned about something, there's always a real reason for it, like a plumbing issue at one of the units or a contractor running late. \n\nI wouldn't just sit around worrying about... well, nothing. That just seems like a silly waste of energy, doesn't it? I always try to be very practical. If there's no obvious reason to fret, I just go about my day and focus on what needs to get done. I definitely wouldn't want you to think I'm the sort of person who just imagines problems out of thin air. I try to be very sensible about it all. \n\nI hope that's the right way to handle things? I just want to be sure I'm doing okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things more days than not?\n\nPatient: More days than not? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I mean, sure, there are days when the phone is ringing off the hook and a tenant has a leaky roof, and I might feel a little... well, a little tense, just wanting to make sure it all gets sorted out. \n\nBut most days? I think if you go around worrying most of the week, it just drags you down, and I really try my best not to let that happen. Well, um... I haven't actually been stepping on the scale much, so I don't have an exact number for you. I hope that's alright, I know I should probably keep better track of things like that for my health.
